```python
def traceable_random_fps(xyz, npoint):
    B, N, _ = xyz.shape
    # Generate random noise for each point
    noise = torch.rand(B, N, device=xyz.device)
    # Sort noise to get random order (deterministic given noise)
    _, indices = torch.sort(noise, dim=-1)
    # Take first npoint indices
    indices = indices[:, :npoint]
    return indices.contiguous().long()
```

```json
{
  "filePath": "/home/wangpeng/CODE/HPENet_v2-main/deploy/onnx_backend.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nPure PyTorch implementations of custom CUDA operations for ONNX export.\n\nAll functions are traceable by torch.jit.trace / torch.onnx.export.\nReplaces the 5 custom CUDA ops from openpoints/cpp/pointnet2_batch/:\n\n    furthest_point_sample → traceable_random_fps\n    ball_query            → traceable_ball_query\n    grouping_operation    → traceable_grouping_operation\n    three_nn              → (inside traceable_three_interpolation)\n    three_interpolate     → (inside traceable_three_interpolation)\n\"\"\"\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\n\n\ndef traceable_random_fps(xyz, npoint):\n    \"\"\"Random sampling to replace FPS (ONNX traceable).\n\n    Args:\n        xyz: (B, N, 3) point coordinates\n        npoint: int, number of points to sample (can be dynamic/traced)\n\n    Returns:\n        idx: (B, npoint) indices, dtype=long\n    \"\"\"\n    B, N, _ = xyz.shape\n    idx = torch.randint(0, N, (B, npoint), device=xyz.device, dtype=torch.long)\n    return idx\n\n\ndef traceable_ball_query(radius, nsample, xyz, new_xyz):\n    \"\"\"Ball query using torch.cdist + topk (ONNX traceable).\n\n    Args:\n        radius: float, ball radius (must be a constant)\n        nsample: int, maximum number of neighbors (must be a constant)\n        xyz: (B, N, 3) support point coordinates\n        new_xyz: (B, npoint, 3) query point coordinates\n\n    Returns:\n        idx: (B, npoint, nsample) neighbor indices, dtype=long.\n             Out-of-radius positions are filled with index 0.\n    \"\"\"\n    B, N, _ = xyz.shape\n    npoint = new_xyz.shape[1]\n\n    dist = torch.cdist(new_xyz, xyz)  # (B, npoint, N)\n\n    large_val = torch.tensor(1e10, dtype=dist.dtype, device=dist.device)\n    dist_masked = torch.where(dist <= radius, dist, large_val)\n\n    _, idx = torch.topk(dist_masked, k=nsample, dim=-1, largest=False)  # (B, npoint, nsample)\n\n    # Reset out-of-radius entries to 0\n    valid_count = (dist <= radius).sum(dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # (B, npoint, 1)\n    pos_range = torch.arange(nsample, device=xyz.device).view(1, 1, nsample)\n    invalid_mask = pos_range >= valid_count  # (B, npoint, nsample)\n    idx = torch.where(invalid_mask, torch.zeros_like(idx), idx)\n\n    return idx.long()\n\n\ndef traceable_grouping_operation(features, idx):\n    \"\"\"Group features by indices using torch.gather (ONNX traceable).\n\n    Args:\n        features: (B, C, N) feature tensor\n        idx: (B, npoint, nsample) or (B, npoint) index tensor\n\n    Returns:\n        grouped: (B, C, npoint, nsample) or (B, C, npoint)\n    \"\"\"\n    idx_flat = idx.reshape(idx.shape[0], -1)  # (B, npoint * nsample)\n    idx_flat = idx_flat.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, features.shape[1], -1)  # (B, C, npoint*nsample)\n    grouped = torch.gather(features, 2, idx_flat.to(torch.int64))\n    return grouped.reshape(idx.shape[0], features.shape[1], *idx.shape[1:])\n\n\ndef traceable_three_interpolation(unknown_xyz, known_xyz, know_feat):\n    \"\"\"Three-nearest-neighbor linear interpolation (ONNX traceable).\n\n    Replaces the combined three_nn + three_interpolate CUDA pipeline\n    with pure torch.cdist + gather.\n\n    Args:\n        unknown_xyz: (B, N, 3)  target point coordinates\n        known_xyz:   (B, M, 3)  source point coordinates\n        know_feat:   (B, C, M)  source features\n\n    Returns:\n        interpolated: (B, C, N)\n    \"\"\"\n    B, C, M = know_feat.shape\n    _, N, _ = unknown_xyz.shape\n\n    # Compute pairwise distances\n    dist = torch.cdist(unknown_xyz, known_xyz)  # (B, N, M)\n\n    # Get 3 nearest neighbors\n    dist_top3, idx_top3 = torch.topk(dist, k=3, dim=-1, largest=False)  # (B, N, 3)\n\n    # Compute inverse distance weights\n    dist_recip = 1.0 / (dist_top3 + 1e-8)  # (B, N, 3)\n    norm = torch.sum(dist_recip, dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # (B, N, 1)\n    weight = dist_recip / norm  # (B, N, 3)\n\n    # Gather 3 neighbor features: (B, C, M) → (B, C, N, 3)\n    idx_flat = idx_top3.reshape(B, -1)  # (B, N * 3)\n    idx_flat = idx_flat.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, C, -1)  # (B, C, N * 3)\n    gathered = torch.gather(know_feat, 2, idx_flat.to(torch.int64))  # (B, C, N * 3)\n    gathered = gathered.reshape(B, C, N, 3)  # (B, C, N, 3)\n\n    # Weighted sum: (B, C, N, 3) * (B, 1, N, 3) → sum over dim=-1 → (B, C, N)\n    weight = weight.unsqueeze(1)  # (B, 1, N, 3)\n    interpolated = torch.sum(gathered * weight, dim=-1)  # (B, C, N)\n\n    return interpolated\n\n\ndef patch_model_for_onnx(model):\n    \"\"\"Replace custom CUDA ops with pure-PyTorch equivalents in-place.\n\n    Patches both:\n    1. Module-level function references (resolved at call time)\n    2. SetAbstraction.sample_fn attributes (captured at __init__ time)\n\n    Args:\n        model: a BaseSeg model with HPENetV2Encoder + HPENetV2Decoder\n\n    Returns:\n        model: the same model (modified in-place)\n    \"\"\"\n    import openpoints.models.backbone.hpenetv2 as hpenetv2_mod\n    import openpoints.models.layers.group as group_mod\n\n    # Patch module-level functions resolved at call time\n    hpenetv2_mod.grouping_operation = traceable_grouping_operation\n    hpenetv2_mod.three_interpolation = traceable_three_interpolation\n    group_mod.ball_query = traceable_ball_query\n    group_mod.grouping_operation = traceable_grouping_operation\n\n    # Walk model and replace sample_fn (captured at __init__ time)\n    patched_count = 0\n    for _name, module in model.named_modules():\n        if hasattr(module, 'sample_fn'):\n            module.sample_fn = traceable_random_fps\n            patched_count += 1\n\n    print(f\"  Patched {patched_count} SetAbstraction.sample_fn: FPS → random sampling\")\n    return model\n"
}
```

Also, I need to handle the `get_features_by_keys` function - actually, the model's forward expects a dict with 'pos' and 'x' keys. The 'x' is already the combined features (4 channels).

For the ONNX export wrapper, the model takes {'pos': pos, 'x': x}. I'll create a wrapper that accepts (pos, x) as separate tensors.

Let me also think about whether I should include the `get_features_by_keys` call in the ONNX model or keep it outside.

Looking at the test code flow:
1. `data['x'] = feat_part` (raw features, 3 channels)
2. `data['heights'] = z_coord` (1 channel)
3. Normalize both
4. `data['x'] = get_features_by_keys(data, 'x,heights')` → combines to 4 channels
5. `model(data)` → model receives {'pos': pos, 'x': combined_features}

So the model input is already the combined 4-channel features. I'll keep this outside the ONNX model.

For the ONNX wrapper:
```python
class HPENetONNXWrapper(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, base_seg_model):
        super().__init__()
        self.model = base_seg_model
    
    def forward(self, pos, x):
        return self.model({'pos': pos, 'x': x})
```

1. Load ONNX model with onnxruntime

5. Run inference per sub-cloud
6. Merge results with scatter mean

Let me write this carefully, following the exact same logic as in examples/segmentation/main.py:642-707.

Key flow:
```python
for data_path in data_list:
    coord, feat, label, idx_points, voxel_idx, reverse_idx_part, reverse_idx = load_data(data_path, cfg)
    
    for idx_subcloud in range(len(idx_points)):
        idx_part = idx_points[idx_subcloud]
        coord_part = coord[idx_part]
        coord_part -= coord_part.min(0)
        feat_part = feat[idx_part]
        
        data = {'pos': coord_part}
        data['x'] = feat_part
        
        # Pipe transform (val: PointsToTensor, PointCloudXYZAlign)
        data = pipe_transform(data)
        
        # Add heights
        data['heights'] = coord_part[:, gravity_dim:gravity_dim+1]
        
        # Normalize
        data['x'] = (data['x'] - feat_mean) / feat_std.clamp(min=1e-5)
        data['heights'] = (data['heights'] - z_mean) / z_std.clamp(min=1e-5)
        
        # Unsqueeze
        data['x'] = data['x'].unsqueeze(0)
        data['pos'] = data['pos'].unsqueeze(0)
        data['heights'] = data['heights'].unsqueeze(0)
        
        # Combine features
        data['x'] = get_features_by_keys(data, 'x,heights')
        
        # Model inference
        logits = model(data)
```

The pipe transform does:
- PointsToTensor: converts numpy arrays to torch tensors
- PointCloudXYZAlign: subtracts min coordinate

Since coord_part is already shifted (coord_part -= coord_part.min(0)), PointCloudXYZAlign is effectively a no-op.

```python
def torch_grouping_operation(features, idx):
    all_idx = idx.reshape(idx.shape[0], -1)

    grouped_features = features.gather(2, all_idx)
```

OK, let me also think about ONNX compatibility of specific ops:
- `torch.cdist` → ONNX opset 16: supported (maps to a series of ReduceSum, Sqrt etc.)
- `torch.topk` → ONNX: supported, but k must be constant (cannot be dynamic)
- `torch.where` → ONNX: supported
- `torch.gather` → ONNX: supported
- `torch.randint` → ONNX: supported through RandomUniform
- `torch.sum` → ONNX: supported
- `torch.mean` → ONNX: supported

The key concern is `torch.topk(k=nsample)` where `nsample` is a Python constant (specified in config). Since it's a Python-level constant, the tracer will see it as constant, so ONNX should handle it fine.

Similarly, `torch.topk(k=3)` in `traceable_three_interpolation` is constant.

Actual best approach: **pad the support set** so it always has at least nsample points. We can duplicate the last point:

```python
def traceable_ball_query(radius, nsample, xyz, new_xyz):
    B, N, _ = xyz.shape
    npoint = new_xyz.shape[1]
    
    # Ensure N >= nsample by padding (needed for topk)
    if N < nsample:
        pad = nsample - N
        xyz_padded = torch.cat([xyz, xyz[:, :1, :].expand(-1, pad, -1)], dim=1)
        N_padded = nsample
    else:
        xyz_padded = xyz
        N_padded = N
    
    dist = torch.cdist(new_xyz, xyz_padded)
    large_val = torch.tensor(1e10, dtype=dist.dtype, device=dist.device)
    # Only mask by radius for original points
    dist_masked = torch.where(dist <= radius, dist, large_val)
    
    _, idx = torch.topk(dist_masked, k=nsample, dim=-1, largest=False)
    
    # Reset entries beyond original N
    valid_mask = torch.arange(nsample, device=xyz.device).view(1, 1, nsample) < N
    idx = torch.where(valid_mask, idx, torch.zeros_like(idx))
    
    # Reset out-of-radius entries
    valid_count = (dist[:, :, :N] <= radius).sum(dim=-1, keepdim=True)
    pos_range = torch.arange(nsample, device=xyz.device).view(1, 1, nsample)
    invalid_mask = pos_range >= valid_count
    idx = torch.where(invalid_mask, torch.zeros_like(idx), idx)
    
    return idx.long()
```

Wait, this still has the problem of the `if N < nsample` branch. During tracing, `N` is a traced value, and Python's `if` won't trace correctly.

For ONNX tracing, I can't use Python if/else with dynamic conditions. I need to use `torch.where` or similar.

Let me try a fully vectorized approach without branching:

```python
def traceable_ball_query(radius, nsample, xyz, new_xyz):
    B, N, _ = xyz.shape
    npoint = new_xyz.shape[1]
    
    # Compute pairwise distances
    dist = torch.cdist(new_xyz, xyz)  # (B, npoint, N)
    
    # Mask out points beyond radius
    large_val = torch.tensor(1e10, dtype=dist.dtype, device=dist.device)
    dist_masked = torch.where(dist <= radius, dist, large_val)
    
    # Sort distances and take first nsample
    # If N >= nsample: works normally
    # If N < nsample: we need to handle this
    # Solution: always use topk with k = min(nsample, N)
    # But for ONNX, k must be constant. So we can't use dynamic k.
    
    # Alternative: use argsort and slice (sort IS ONNX-compatible with dynamic inputs)
    _, sorted_idx = torch.sort(dist_masked, dim=-1)  # (B, npoint, N)
    
    # Take up to nsample elements
    # Since nsample is constant, slicing with :nsample works in ONNX
    # But if N < nsample, we get fewer elements → shape mismatch
    # We need to handle this
    
    # Pad sorted_idx to have at least nsample columns
    # Use the first column as padding (index 0)
    if N < nsample:
        # Concatenate padding
        pad_len = nsample - N
        padding = sorted_idx[:, :, :1].expand(-1, -1, pad_len)
        sorted_idx = torch.cat([sorted_idx, padding], dim=-1)
    
    idx = sorted_idx[:, :, :nsample]  # (B, npoint, nsample)
    
    # Reset padded entries (beyond original N) to 0
    valid_pos_mask = torch.arange(nsample, device=xyz.device).view(1, 1, nsample) < N
    idx = torch.where(valid_pos_mask, idx, torch.zeros_like(idx))
    
    # Also reset entries with no valid neighbors within radius to 0
    valid_in_radius = (dist <= radius).sum(dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # (B, npoint, 1)
    pos_range = torch.arange(nsample, device=xyz.device).view(1, 1, nsample)
    out_of_radius = pos_range >= valid_in_radius
    idx = torch.where(out_of_radius, torch.zeros_like(idx), idx)
    
    return idx.long()
```

Hmm, the `if N < nsample` branch is still problematic for tracing.

OK let me think about this differently. For the ONNX export, the dummy input has N points. During tracing, all dimension sizes are known. The `if N < nsample` check will be evaluated once with the concrete value of N. If N >= nsample (which is typical for most stages), the `if` branch is taken with the concrete value. The traced graph will include the padding logic, but since the condition is evaluated at trace time, only one branch is traced.

BUT the problem is: the exported ONNX model will have the traced graph hardcoded. If at inference time the actual N is different (smaller), the model might fail because the branch wasn't taken.

However, for ONNX with `dynamic_axes`, the N dimension IS dynamic. The traced graph needs to work for any N. The `if N < nsample` creates a conditional that's evaluated at trace time, not at runtime. So if the condition is False during trace (N >= nsample), the padding code is never included in the graph, and if later N < nsample, the model would fail.

This means we need a DIFFERENT approach that doesn't use Python if/else with dynamic conditions.

The key challenge: `torch.topk` requires constant k for ONNX export. But we need dynamic behavior.

Alternative approach: always pad the support set to at least nsample points. Use `torch.where` to conditionally add padding:

```python
# Always ensure there are at least nsample support points
B, N, _ = xyz.shape
nsample_const = nsample  # constant from config

# Create padding: repeat the first point
pad_points = xyz[:, :1, :]  # (B, 1, 3)
# We need to pad to nsample if N < nsample
# Always create full padding, then use torch.where to select
full_pad = pad_points.repeat(1, nsample_const, 1)  # (B, nsample, 3)
# Concatenate original + full_pad: this ensures N+nsample >= nsample
xyz_extended = torch.cat([xyz, full_pad], dim=1)  # (B, N+nsample, 3)
N_ext = N + nsample_const
```

Wait, this always adds nsample padding points regardless. Then:
```python
dist = torch.cdist(new_xyz, xyz_extended)  # (B, npoint, N+nsample)
# Mask padded points to large distance
# Original points: dist <= radius → keep
# Padded points: always large
dist_masked = dist.clone()
dist_masked[:, :, N:] = large_val  # padded points are far away
dist_masked = torch.where(dist_masked <= radius, dist_masked, large_val)

# Now topk(k=nsample) always works because N+nsample >= nsample
_, idx = torch.topk(dist_masked, k=nsample_const, dim=-1, largest=False)
# Reset indices that point to padded positions to 0
padded_mask = idx >= N
idx = torch.where(padded_mask, torch.zeros_like(idx), idx)
```

But `dist_masked[:, :, N:] = large_val` modifies a slice based on dynamic N. Does this work in ONNX?

Actually, slicing with dynamic start doesn't work well in ONNX. `[:, :, N:]` where N is dynamic creates a `Slice` op with dynamic start, which IS supported in ONNX opset 11+.

```python
# Create extended xyz
extended_xyz = torch.cat([xyz, xyz[:, :1, :].expand(-1, nsample_const, -1)], dim=1)
N_ext = N + nsample_const  # dynamic

# Compute distances
dist = torch.cdist(new_xyz, extended_xyz)  # (B, npoint, N+nsample)

# Create mask: original points within radius → keep distance
# Padded points (position >= N) → set to large_val
# Points beyond radius → set to large_val
pos_idx = torch.arange(N_ext, device=xyz.device).view(1, 1, N_ext)  # (1, 1, N_ext)
is_padded = pos_idx >= N  # (1, 1, N_ext) → broadcast to (B, npoint, N_ext)
in_radius = dist <= radius
valid = in_radius & (~is_padded)  # original + within radius
dist_masked = torch.where(valid, dist, torch.tensor(1e10, device=dist.device, dtype=dist.dtype))

# Now N_ext >= nsample always (N + nsample >= nsample)
_, idx = torch.topk(dist_masked, k=nsample_const, dim=-1, largest=False)
# Reset indices >= N to 0
idx = torch.where(idx >= N, torch.zeros_like(idx), idx)
```

1. Always pads xyz with nsample extra points (duplicates of first point)

3. Uses constant k for topk (ONNX compatible)

But the masking for the distance computation requires something different. Let me think again...

For the distance masking:
1. Original approach: take all extended points, compute dist, mask padded ones to large_val
2. Problem: creating the position-based mask requires knowing which positions are padded

Actually, there's a simpler approach: since we know padded positions are `[N, N+nsample)`, and N is dynamic, I can do:
- Compute distance for all extended points
- Create a mask based on position: for each position j, j >= N means padded
- But creating a position index (0 to N+nsample-1) requires arange with dynamic bound

Hmm, OK. Let me think of yet another approach.

What if I don't use position-based masking at all? Instead:
1. Set the distance of padded points to 1e10
2. Set the distance of out-of-radius points to 1e10
3. Use topk with k=nsample

For step 1, I can achieve this by making the padded points have very large coordinates (like 1e10), so their distance will naturally be large. But this will also affect the cdist computation time.

Actually, simpler: after computing dist, I can mask the padded columns. Since I know the padded range is [N, N+nsample), I can use a mask created with operations on the indices. But without arange...

Wait, I can create a constant mask! Since nsample is constant, I know exactly how many padded positions there are relative to N. For the extended tensor with shape (B, N+nsample, 3), the padded positions are always the last nsample entries.

I can create a simple mask using slicing:
```python
# dist shape: (B, npoint, N+nsample_const)
# Last nsample_const columns are padding
# Create mask: first N columns are original, last nsample_const are padded
# But we don't know N dynamically...

# OK, instead of masking by position, let me use a different trick:
# Make the padded points have very different coordinates so cdist gives large values
# e.g., set padded xyz to contain NaN-like large values
large_xyz = xyz[:, :1, :] + 1e6  # very far from origin
extended_xyz = torch.cat([xyz, large_xyz.expand(-1, nsample_const, -1)], dim=1)
dist = torch.cdist(new_xyz, extended_xyz)  # padded points have large distance
dist_masked = torch.where(dist <= radius, dist, large_val)
```

This way, padded points naturally have large distances (>1e6 + offset), which would be > radius, so they'd be automatically masked by `dist <= radius`. No position-based masking needed!

But wait, `xyz[:, :1, :] + 1e6` creates padded points far away. Their distance from any query point would be at least ~1e6. If radius is small (like 0.1-1.6), then `dist <= radius` would be False for all padded points, and they'd be set to large_val. Then topk would prefer original points.

However, if N < nsample and all original points are beyond radius, the padded points would still have large distances (1e10 from the masking). topk would select a mix of original and padded points. The padded point indices would be in range [N, N+nsample). We can then check `idx >= N` to reset them to 0.

This approach works! Let me also consider the case where there ARE valid neighbors. If N >= nsample and some are within radius, topk selects the nsample best original points. Good.

If N < nsample and some ARE within radius, topk selects those valid original points first, then fills with padded points. Good, we then reset padded indices to 0.

Let me implement this cleaner version.

Actually, let me simplify even further. Instead of padding the coordinates, I can pad the distance matrix:

```python
def traceable_ball_query(radius, nsample, xyz, new_xyz):
    B, N, _ = xyz.shape
    npoint = new_xyz.shape[1]
    
    dist = torch.cdist(new_xyz, xyz)  # (B, npoint, N)
    large_val = torch.tensor(1e10, dtype=dist.dtype, device=dist.device)
    
    # Mask out-of-radius points
    dist_masked = torch.where(dist <= radius, dist, large_val)
    
    # Pad dist_masked with extra columns of large_val so shape[-1] >= nsample
    # This ensures topk never fails with "k out of range"
    pad_len = nsample  # always add nsample padding columns
    padding = large_val.expand(B, npoint, pad_len)
    dist_padded = torch.cat([dist_masked, padding], dim=-1)  # (B, npoint, N+nsample)
    
    # topk with constant k=nsample always works since N+nsample >= nsample
    _, idx = torch.topk(dist_padded, k=nsample, dim=-1, largest=False)
    
    # Reset indices that point to padded columns (>= N) to 0
    idx = torch.where(idx >= N, torch.zeros_like(idx), idx)
    
    # Also reset entries that correspond to fully invalid queries
    # (no original point within radius)
    valid_in_radius = (dist <= radius).sum(dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # (B, npoint, 1)
    pos_range = torch.arange(nsample, device=xyz.device).view(1, 1, nsample)
    out_of_radius_mask = pos_range >= valid_in_radius
    idx = torch.where(out_of_radius_mask, torch.zeros_like(idx), idx)
    
    return idx.long()
```

This is much cleaner! The `torch.arange(nsample)` uses a constant nsample, which is ONNX-compatible.

But there's still one issue: `idx >= N` where N is dynamic. This is fine in ONNX — it's a comparison op with a dynamic value.

And `torch.where(idx >= N, ...)` is also fine — ONNX supports `Where` with dynamic conditions.

Let me update `traceable_ball_query` with this implementation.

The export test reveals a bug: `topk(k=nsample)` fails when N < nsample in deep stages. Let me fix the `traceable_ball_query` to handle this case by adding distance padding.

```
65: def traceable_grouping_operation(features, idx):
66:     """Group features by indices using torch.gather (ONNX traceable).
67: 
68:     Args:
69:         features: (B, C, N) feature tensor
70:         idx: (B, npoint, nsample) or (B, npoint) index tensor
71:

74:     """

76:     idx_flat = idx_flat.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, features.shape[1], -1)  # (B, C, npoint*nsample)
77:     grouped = torch.gather(features, 2, idx_flat.to(torch.int64))

81: def traceable_three_interpolation(unknown_xyz, known_xyz, know_feat):
82:     """Three-nearest-neighbor linear interpolation (ONNX traceable).
83: 
84:     Replaces the combined three_nn + three_interpolate CUDA pipeline
85:     with pure torch.cdist + gather.
86: 
87:     Args:
88:         unknown_xyz: (B, N, 3)  target point coordinates
89:         known_xyz:   (B, M, 3)  source point coordinates
90:         know_feat:   (B, C, M)  source features
91: 
92:     Returns:
93:         interpolated: (B, C, N)
94:     """
95:     B, C, M = know_feat.shape
96:     _, N, _ = unknown_xyz.shape
97: 
98:     # Compute pairwise distances
99:     dist = torch.cdist(unknown_xyz, known_xyz)  # (B, N, M)
100: 
101:     # Get 3 nearest neighbors
102:     dist_top3, idx_top3 = torch.topk(dist, k=3, dim=-1, largest=False)  # (B, N, 3)
103: 
104:     # Compute inverse distance weights
105:     dist_recip = 1.0 / (dist_top3 + 1e-8)  # (B, N, 3)
106:     norm = torch.sum(dist_recip, dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # (B, N, 1)
107:     weight = dist_recip / norm  # (B, N, 3)
108: 
109:     # Gather 3 neighbor features: (B, C, M) → (B, C, N, 3)
110:     idx_flat = idx_top3.reshape(B, -1)  # (B, N * 3)
111:     idx_flat = idx_flat.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, C, -1)  # (B, C, N * 3)
112:     gathered = torch.gather(know_feat, 2, idx_flat.to(torch.int64))  # (B, C, N * 3)
113:     gathered = gathered.reshape(B, C, N, 3)  # (B, C, N, 3)
114: 
115:     # Weighted sum: (B, C, N, 3) * (B, 1, N, 3) → sum over dim=-1 → (B, C, N)
116:     weight = weight.unsqueeze(1)  # (B, 1, N, 3)
117:     interpolated = torch.sum(gathered * weight, dim=-1)  # (B, C, N)
118: 
119:     return interpolated
120: 
121:

124: 
125:     Patches both:
126:     1. Module-level function references (resolved at call time)
127:     2. SetAbstraction.sample_fn attributes (captured at __init__ time)

136:     import openpoints.models.layers.group as group_mod
137: 
138:     # Patch module-level functions resolved at call time

140:     hpenetv2_mod.three_interpolation = traceable_three_interpolation
141:     group_mod.ball_query = traceable_ball_query
142:     group_mod.grouping_operation = traceable_grouping_operation
143: 
144:     # Walk model and replace sample_fn (captured at __init__ time)

146:     for _name, module in model.named_modules():

150:
```

_Thinking:_

The test forward passed (output shape correct, no NaN/Inf). But the ONNX export fails with `Unsupported: ONNX export of instance_norm for unknown channel size`.

This is a known issue: `torch.nn.InstanceNorm1d` (and other InstanceNorm variants) don't support ONNX export with dynamic channel dimensions. The model uses `norm='in'` which means `InstanceNorm1d`.

The issue is in the `SegHead` layer. The head uses `InstanceNorm1d` for normalization. Let me check...

Looking at the error trace:
```
openpoints.models.segmentation.seg_head.SegHead::head
  torch.nn.modules.container.Sequential::head.0
    openpoints.models.layers.conv.Conv1d::head.0.0
```

The head is built with:
```python
heads.append(create_convblock1d(mlps[i], mlps[i + 1], norm_args=norm_args, act_args=act_args))
```

The `norm_args` is `{'norm': 'in'}` from config. This creates `Conv1d + InstanceNorm1d + ReLU` blocks.

The error says "instance_norm for unknown channel size". The channel size is dynamic because of the dynamic input. InstanceNorm1d needs the channel dimension to be known at export time.

Solutions:
1. Change the head to use BatchNorm1d instead of InstanceNorm1d for ONNX export
2. Export with fixed channel dimensions
3. Replace InstanceNorm1d with a custom implementation that works with ONNX
4. Remove normalization from the head entirely for ONNX export

Solution 1 is the simplest. The head only has Conv1d + InstanceNorm1d + ReLU. For inference, InstanceNorm1d and BatchNorm1d behave similarly (both normalize per-channel). The key difference is that InstanceNorm normalizes per-sample, while BatchNorm uses running statistics. But for a single-sample inference (batch_size=1), they're equivalent.

However, changing norm from 'in' to 'bn' would change the behavior. For single-sample, InstanceNorm is equivalent to LayerNorm on the spatial dimension. Let me think...

Actually, the simplest solution is to replace InstanceNorm1d with Identity for the ONNX export. Or better: use a custom InstanceNorm1d-like op that can be exported.

Since our `x` input has dynamic npoints but fixed channels (4), the channel dimension should be known...

The input has symbolic dimensions (Float(*, *, *, ...)). The channel dimension is `*` which is symbolic. This means the tracer couldn't determine the channel size because it depends on dynamic computation.

Then `torch.onnx.export` tries to handle this by treating it as a constant branch, but the channel dimension becomes unknown.

This module computes the same operation using basic\n    PyTorch ops (mean, var, add, mul) which all have ONNX support.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, num_features, eps=1e-5, momentum=0.1, track_running_stats=False):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.num_features = num_features\n        self.eps = eps\n        \n        self.weight = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(num_features))\n        self.bias = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(num_features))\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        # x: (B, C, N)\n        mean = x.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # (B, C, 1)\n        var = ((x - mean) ** 2).mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # (B, C, 1)\n        x_norm = (x - mean) / torch.sqrt(var + self.eps)\n        return x_norm * self.weight.view(1, -1, 1) + self.bias.view(1, -1, 1)",
```json
  "oldString": "import torch\nimport torch.nn as nn"
}
```

## Assistant (Build · DeepSeek V4 Pro · 36.4s)

_Thinking:_

OK, a different error now. The InstanceNorm replacement seems to have worked (51 instances replaced), but there's a shape mismatch. The error happens at:
```
File ".../position_encoding.py", line 41, in forward
    feature_new =  self.conv(xyz)
```

The `conv` is a Sequential of:
```
create_convblock2d(3, in_channels//4, ...)
create_convblock2d(in_channels//4, in_channels, ...)
```

The first conv2d has 3 input channels and 8 output channels (32 // 4). The second has 8 input and 32 output channels.

The problem: `ONNXInstanceNorm1d` expects input of shape (B, C, N), but the HPE module uses **Conv2d** which produces output of shape (B, C, H, W) where H and W are spatial dimensions. The InstanceNorm2d that was replaced expects (B, C, H, W).

My `ONNXInstanceNorm1d` does:
```python
mean = x.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # mean over last dim
```

For a 4D tensor (B, C, H, W), this would compute mean over W only, not over (H, W). But InstanceNorm2d should normalize over (H, W).

I need to fix `ONNXInstanceNorm1d` to handle 2D and 3D cases (or create separate versions).

More precisely:
- InstanceNorm1d: input (B, C, L), normalize over L
- InstanceNorm2d: input (B, C, H, W), normalize over H*W
- InstanceNorm3d: input (B, C, D, H, W), normalize over D*H*W

For my `ONNXInstanceNorm1d`, I should normalize over all spatial dimensions (all dims after C).

Let me fix this to handle any number of spatial dimensions.

`ONNXInstanceNorm1d` doesn't handle InstanceNorm2d (4D tensors used in HPE). Let me fix it to handle arbitrary spatial dimensions:

**Tool: edit**

**Input:**
```json
{
  "filePath": "/home/wangpeng/CODE/HPENet_v2-main/deploy/onnx_backend.py",
  "newString": "class ONNXInstanceNorm1d(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"InstanceNorm replacement that is ONNX-exportable.\n\n    Handles 1D (B,C,N), 2D (B,C,H,W), and 3D (B,C,D,H,W) inputs\n    by normalizing over all spatial dimensions (dims 2..).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, num_features, eps=1e-5, momentum=0.1, track_running_stats=False):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.num_features = num_features\n        self.eps = eps\n        \n        self.weight = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(num_features))\n        self.bias = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(num_features))\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        # x: (B, C, ...) — normalize over all spatial dims\n        ndim = x.dim()\n        # Compute mean and var across spatial dims (2 to ndim-1)\n        spatial_dims = list(range(2, ndim))\n        mean = x.mean(dim=spatial_dims, keepdim=True)  # (B, C, 1, ...)\n        var = ((x - mean) ** 2).mean(dim=spatial_dims, keepdim=True)\n        x_norm = (x - mean) / torch.sqrt(var + self.eps)\n        # Reshape weight/bias for broadcasting\n        shape = [1, -1] + [1] * (ndim - 2)\n        return x_norm * self.weight.view(*shape) + self.bias.view(*shape)",
  "oldString": "class ONNXInstanceNorm1d(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"InstanceNorm1d replacement that is ONNX-exportable.\n\n    PyTorch's native InstanceNorm1d cannot be exported to ONNX with dynamic\n    channel dimensions.  This module computes the same operation using basic\n    PyTorch ops (mean, var, add, mul) which all have ONNX support.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, num_features, eps=1e-5, momentum=0.1, track_running_stats=False):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.num_features = num_features\n        self.eps = eps\n        \n        self.weight = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(num_features))\n        self.bias = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(num_features))\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        # x: (B, C, N)\n        mean = x.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # (B, C, 1)\n        var = ((x - mean) ** 2).mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)  # (B, C, 1)\n        x_norm = (x - mean) / torch.sqrt(var + self.eps)\n        return x_norm * self.weight.view(1, -1, 1) + self.bias.view(1, -1, 1)"
}
```

Let me think about whether to fix the InvResMLP_block issue. Looking at the code:
```python
if f.shape[-1] == identity.shape[-1] and self.use_res:
    f += identity
```

This check uses `.shape[-1]` to compare spatial dimensions. In the traced graph, both tensors always have the same last dimension (both are operated on by stride-1 layers). During ONNX tracing, this becomes a constant `True`, so the traced graph includes the `f += identity`. This should be correct for all inputs since the spatial dimensions will always match for these layers.

The TracerWarning is just a warning, not an error. We can safely ignore it.

## Assistant (Build · DeepSeek V4 Pro · 15.1s)

All tasks completed. Let me present the final summary.

## 部署完成

### 生成的文件

| 文件 | 功能 |
|------|------|
| `deploy/onnx_backend.py` | 5 个 CUDA 算子的纯 PyTorch 替代 + InstanceNorm ONNX 兼容替换 + `patch_model_for_onnx()` |
| `deploy/onnx_export.py` | 加载 checkPoint → 构建模型 → 替换算子 → 导出 ONNX |
| `deploy/onnx_inference.py` | onnxruntime 完整推理管线（PLY读取 + 体素化 + 归一化 + 投票合并） |
| `deploy/onnx_model.onnx` | 导出的 ONNX 模型（11.7 MB） |

### 技术要点

- **FPS → 随机采样**: `torch.rand` + `argsort` 替代最远点采样（精度损失 < 2%）
- **ball_query**: `torch.cdist` + `topk`，距离矩阵 padding 处理 N < nsample 边界情况
- **grouping_operation**: `torch.gather` 替代 CUDA 聚集操作
- **three_interpolation**: `torch.cdist` + gather + 加权求和
- **InstanceNorm**: 使用基础 PyTorch 算子手写实现，兼容 ONNX 动态 shape

### 验证结果（5 个测试文件）

- ONNX vs PyTorch 预测一致性：**88-94%**
- 准确率差值：< 2%（来自 FPS→随机采样的 tradeoff）
- ONNX 模型文件大小：11.7 MB，CPU 推理每文件 ~1.5s

### 使用方式

```bash
# 导出 ONNX（已完成）
python deploy/onnx_export.py

# 推理验证
python deploy/onnx_inference.py --num_files 10
```

## TensorRT 部署方案

### 文件结构

```
deploy/
├── onnx_model.onnx                    # 已有 ONNX 模型
├── onnx_backend.py                    # 已有 ONNX 兼容层
├── onnx_export.py                     # 已有 ONNX 导出
├── onnx_inference.py                  # 已有 ONNX 推理
├── trt_build.py                       # NEW: ONNX → TensorRT engine
├── trt_inference.py                   # NEW: TensorRT 推理管线
├── trt_utils.py                       # NEW: 共享工具（环境检测、GPU 内存管理）
├── trt_model_fp32.engine              # 构建后生成：FP32 engine
└── trt_model_fp16.engine              # 构建后生成：FP16 engine
```

### trt_build.py — 构建 TensorRT Engine

**流程**：
1. 加载 ONNX 模型 (`deploy/onnx_model.onnx`)
2. 创建 `tensorrt.Builder` + `tensorrt.NetworkDefinition` + `tensorrt.OnnxParser`
3. 设置动态 shape 优化配置（npoints: min=1024, opt=4096, max=30000）
4. Builder config：设置最大工作空间、FP16 flag（可选）
5. 序列化并保存 `.engine` 文件

**API 调用链**：
```python
builder = tensorrt.Builder(logger)
network = builder.create_network(1 << int(NetworkDefinitionCreationFlag.EXPLICIT_BATCH))
parser = tensorrt.OnnxParser(network, logger)
parser.parse(onnx_model_bytes)

# 动态 shape profile
profile = builder.create_optimization_profile()
profile.set_shape("pos", (1,1024,3), (1,4096,3), (1,30000,3))
profile.set_shape("x",   (1,4,1024), (1,4,4096), (1,4,30000))

config = builder.create_builder_config()
config.add_optimization_profile(profile)
config.set_memory_pool_limit(MemoryPoolType.WORKSPACE, 2 << 30)

if fp16:
    config.set_flag(BuilderFlag.FP16)

engine = builder.build_serialized_network(network, config)
```

### trt_utils.py — 共享工具

**功能**：
- `setup_trt_env()` — 设置 `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` 加载 TensorRT/CUDA 库
- `allocate_gpu_buffers(engine)` — 使用 `torch.empty(..., device='cuda')` 预分配 GPU 缓冲区
- `trt_infer(context, buffers, stream)` — 执行推理并返回结果
- 类型映射：`tensorrt.DataType` ↔ `torch.dtype` ↔ `numpy.dtype`

**GPU 内存管理**（避免依赖 cupy/pycuda）：
```python
class TRTInference:
    def __init__(self, engine_path):
        self.runtime = tensorrt.Runtime(logger)
        with open(engine_path, "rb") as f:
            self.engine = self.runtime.deserialize_cuda_engine(f.read())
        self.context = self.engine.create_execution_context()
        
    def allocate_buffers(self):
        """使用 torch tensor 分配 GPU 内存"""
        self.inputs = {}
        self.outputs = {}
        for i in range(self.engine.num_io_tensors):
            name = self.engine.get_tensor_name(i)
            shape = self.engine.get_tensor_shape(name)
            dtype = trt_to_torch_dtype(self.engine.get_tensor_dtype(name))
            tensor = torch.empty(tuple(shape), dtype=dtype, device='cuda')
            self.inputs[name] = tensor if self.engine.get_tensor_mode(name) == 'INPUT' else None
            self.outputs[name] = tensor if self.engine.get_tensor_mode(name) == 'OUTPUT' else None
            self.context.set_tensor_address(name, tensor.data_ptr())
    
    def infer(self, pos_np, x_np):
        """pos_np: (1, N, 3) float32, x_np: (1, 4, N) float32"""
        # 设置动态 shape
        self.context.set_input_shape("pos", pos_np.shape)
        self.context.set_input_shape("x", x_np.shape)
        
        # 拷贝数据到 GPU
        pos_gpu = torch.from_numpy(pos_np).cuda()
        x_gpu = torch.from_numpy(x_np).cuda()
        
        # 更新 tensor 地址（如果 shape 变化需要重新分配）
        self.context.set_tensor_address("pos", pos_gpu.data_ptr())
        self.context.set_tensor_address("x", x_gpu.data_ptr())
        
        # 分配输出 buffer
        B, _, N = x_gpu.shape
        out_gpu = torch.empty(B, 2, N, dtype=torch.float32, device='cuda')
        self.context.set_tensor_address("output", out_gpu.data_ptr())
        
        # 执行
        stream = torch.cuda.current_stream()
        self.context.execute_async_v3(stream.cuda_stream)
        stream.synchronize()
        
        return out_gpu.cpu().numpy()
```

### trt_inference.py — 推理管线

复用 `onnx_inference.py` 的预处理逻辑（PLY 读取、体素化、归一化、投票合并），仅替换推理后端：

```python
def main():
    # 1. 设置 TRT 环境
    setup_trt_env()
    
    # 2. 加载 TRT engine
    trt = TRTInference("deploy/trt_model_fp32.engine")
    
    # 3. 加载统计量（同 onnx_inference.py）
    feat_mean, feat_std, z_mean, z_std = load_stats(...)
    
    # 4. 遍历 PLY 文件
    for fname in test_files:
        coord, feat, label = load_data_ply(data_path)
        coord, feat, idx_points, ... = preprocess_test(...)
        
        # 5. 体素级推理
        for idx_part in idx_points:
            # 预处理（同 onnx_inference.py）
            pos_batch, x_batch = preprocess_subcloud(coord, feat, idx_part, ...)
            
            # TRT 推理
            logits = trt.infer(pos_batch, x_batch)  # GPU 加速
            ...
        
        # 6. 投票合并
        merged = scatter_mean(all_logits, idx_flat)
```

### 环境配置

TensorRT 库路径需要在脚本中设置：
```python
import os
os.environ['LD_LIBRARY_PATH'] = (
    '/usr/local/TensorRT-8.6.1.6/targets/x86_64-linux-gnu/lib:'
    '/usr/local/cuda-11.8/lib64:' 
    + os.environ.get('LD_LIBRARY_PATH', '')
)
```

### TensorRT 8.6 API 注意事项

- **API 版本**：8.6 使用新的 `set_tensor_address` / `set_input_shape` API，而非旧的 `set_binding_shape` / `enqueue_v2`
- **EXPLICIT_BATCH**：ONNX 模型使用显式 batch 维度，构建时需指定
- **FP16**：L20 GPU 支持 FP16，需在构建时启用 `BuilderFlag.FP16`
- **动态 shape**：每次不同 N 输入时，调用 `set_input_shape` 后执行

**Solution for RandomUniform:**

- Take every k-th point: `torch.arange(0, N, N//npoint)[:npoint]`

- Pre-determine the indices based on N (not really random but deterministic)

Actually, the cleanest approach: re-export the ONNX model with `traceable_random_fps` replaced by a deterministic equivalent:

```python
def traceable_deterministic_fps(xyz, npoint):
    B, N, _ = xyz.shape
    # Deterministic pseudo-random sampling using modular arithmetic
    step = max(1, N // npoint)
    idx = torch.arange(0, npoint, device=xyz.device).unsqueeze(0).expand(B, -1)
    idx = (idx * step) % N
    return idx.long()
```

### Architecture

```
deploy/
├── trt_build.py          # ONNX → TensorRT engine (FP32/FP16)
├── trt_utils.py          # TRT environment, memory management, inference helper
├── trt_inference.py      # Full inference pipeline with TRT backend
├── trt_model_fp32.engine # Generated: FP32 engine
└── trt_model_fp16.engine # Generated: FP16 engine
```

### trt_utils.py

Shared utilities:
1. `setup_trt_env()` - Set LD_LIBRARY_PATH for TRT libs
2. `TRTEngine` class:
   - `__init__(engine_path)`: Load engine from file
   - `create_context()`: Create execution context
   - `get_io_info()`: Return input/output names, shapes, dtypes
   - `allocate_io_tensors()`: Allocate GPU buffers using torch

3. `TRTInferenceSession` class:
   - Wraps engine + context + GPU buffers
   - `run(pos_np, x_np)`: Execute inference with dynamic shapes
   - Handles shape changes between calls
   - Uses torch CUDA stream for async execution

### trt_build.py

```
Usage: python deploy/trt_build.py [--fp16] [--min_n 64] [--opt_n 4096] [--max_n 30000]

Flow:
1. setup_trt_env()
2. Read deploy/onnx_model.onnx
3. Create builder, network, parser
4. Set optimization profile with {min, opt, max} shapes
5. Build engine (FP32 default, FP16 if --fp16)
6. Save as deploy/trt_model_fp{32,16}.engine
```

### trt_inference.py

```
Usage: python deploy/trt_inference.py [--engine PATH] [--fp16] [--num_files N] [--compare]

Flow:
1. setup_trt_env()
2. Load TRT engine
3. Load feature stats
4. Load PyTorch/ONNX model (for comparison)
5. For each test PLY file:
   a. Load data, voxelize, split into sub-clouds
   b. For each sub-cloud:
      - Preprocess (normalize features)
      - TRT inference
   c. Merge results with scatter mean
   d. Compute accuracy vs ground truth
   e. Compare with PyTorch/ONNX if requested
```

### Key API details for TRT 8.6.1

**Building:**
```python
EXPLICIT_BATCH = 1 << int(trt.NetworkDefinitionCreationFlag.EXPLICIT_BATCH)

builder = trt.Builder(trt.Logger(trt.Logger.WARNING))
network = builder.create_network(EXPLICIT_BATCH)
parser = trt.OnnxParser(network, trt.Logger(trt.Logger.WARNING))

with open(onnx_path, 'rb') as f:
    parser.parse(f.read())

profile = builder.create_optimization_profile()
profile.set_shape("pos", (1, min_n, 3), (1, opt_n, 3), (1, max_n, 3))
profile.set_shape("x", (1, 4, min_n), (1, 4, opt_n), (1, 4, max_n))

config = builder.create_builder_config()
config.add_optimization_profile(profile)
config.set_memory_pool_limit(trt.MemoryPoolType.WORKSPACE, 2 << 30)  # 2 GB
if fp16:
    config.set_flag(trt.BuilderFlag.FP16)
    config.set_flag(trt.BuilderFlag.STRICT_TYPES)  # only use FP16 where safe

engine_bytes = builder.build_serialized_network(network, config)
```

**Inference:**
```python
runtime = trt.Runtime(trt.Logger(trt.Logger.WARNING))
engine = runtime.deserialize_cuda_engine(engine_bytes)
context = engine.create_execution_context()

# Dynamic shape: set before each inference
context.set_input_shape("pos", (1, N, 3))
context.set_input_shape("x", (1, 4, N))

# Allocate GPU buffers using torch
import torch
dtype_map = {
    trt.float32: torch.float32,
    trt.float16: torch.float16,
}

# Find I/O tensors
for i in range(engine.num_io_tensors):
    name = engine.get_tensor_name(i)
    mode = engine.get_tensor_mode(name)  # INPUT or OUTPUT
    # dtype = get_tensor_dtype, shape = get_tensor_shape
    # Allocate with torch, set address

# Fill input, run, get output
# H2D copy: torch.from_numpy(pos_np).cuda()
context.set_tensor_address("pos", pos_gpu.data_ptr())
context.set_tensor_address("x", x_gpu.data_ptr())
context.set_tensor_address("output", out_gpu.data_ptr())

stream = torch.cuda.current_stream()
context.execute_async_v3(stream.cuda_stream)
stream.synchronize()
```

### Error handling strategy

1. **Parser errors**: Check `parser.num_errors` after parsing ONNX
2. **Build errors**: Catch `trt.Error` exceptions
3. **Unsupported ops**: Fall back to ONNX Runtime for unsupported ops
4. **Shape mismatch**: Validate input shapes before `set_input_shape`
5. **Memory**: Wrap GPU allocations in try/except for OOM

### Optimization profile design

Based on data analysis:
- Point clouds have ~3000-3500 points
- After voxelization (0.1), sub-clouds have varying sizes
- Voxel grid: roughly 400x500 range → ~20 million potential voxels, but only ~3000 occupied
- Each sub-cloud has 1 point per unique voxel → N = number of unique voxels

Optimization profile:
| Scenario | npoints | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| min | 1 | Smallest possible sub-cloud (single point) |
| opt | 4096 | Typical for our use case (matching ONNX export) |
| max | 30000 | Safety margin for large point clouds |

- Actually, the minimum is determined by the deepest layer. After 4 downsampling stages with stride 4 each, N_min = 4^4 = 256... no wait.

For stage 4 (last): N must be at least stride=4, so N >= 4 at that stage.
Going backwards: 
- Stage 4 input N_4 >= stride * 1 = 4
- Stage 3 input N_3 >= stride * N_4 = 4 * 4 = 16
- Stage 2 input N_2 >= 4 * 16 = 64
- Stage 1 input N_1 >= 4 * 64 = 256

So minimum N is 256. But our ball_query handles N < nsample, so maybe 64 would work with random FPS producing fewer points than stride. Actually, random FPS can produce npoint = N // stride. If N=64 and stride=4, npoint=16. This is fine.

Actually, the critical path is FPS in SetAbstraction: `npoint = p.shape[1] // stride`. If N=1 and stride=4, npoint=0, which is invalid. So minimum N must be >= max(stride) = 4.

But also, ball_query needs at least 1 neighbor. With nsample=32 and padding, even N=1 works.

For safety, I'll set min=64 (conservative). For very small sub-clouds (< 64 points), we can pad or skip OR the caller can handle it differently.

Actually wait, let me reconsider. The user said the voxel_max is null for test mode, meaning sub-clouds CAN be any size. The minimum sub-cloud size depends on the specific voxel grid. With 3000 points and ~3000 unique voxels, there would be exactly 1 point per sub-cloud. So N=1 is possible.

For N=1, the model might fail. We should either:
1. Handle N < min_size by padding
2. Or skip inference for very small clouds

For the TRT plan, I'll recommend padding very small inputs to min_n and trimming the output.

OK, let me now formalize the plan. I'll present a comprehensive, detailed plan.

Key design decision: For the TRT engine build, we need to handle `min_n` carefully. The model internally uses downsampling with stride=4, so the smallest functional N should be checked.

Let me test: what happens when N=1?

Actually, in `SetAbstraction`:
```python
idx = self.sample_fn(p, p.shape[1] // self.stride).long()
```

With N=1 and stride=4: `1 // 4 = 0`. `torch.randint(0, 1, (B, 0))` would produce an empty tensor. Then:
```python
new_p = torch.gather(p, 1, idx.unsqueeze(-1).expand(-1, -1, 3))
```
Gathering with empty indices → empty tensor. Then ball_query with empty query → issues.

So N must be >= 4 for the model to function. For safety, let's set min_n=64.

For the plan, I'll note:
- min_n = 64 (minimum safe input size)
- opt_n = 4096 (typical)
- max_n = 30000 (safety margin for large clouds)

The inference pipeline should handle sub-clouds with N < 64 by either:
1. Padding them to 64 points (duplicating the last point)
2. Or falling back to ONNX Runtime for small clouds

Actually, I think the simplest and most robust approach is to just pad very small inputs to `min_n` on the Python side before feeding to TRT. This doesn't affect accuracy because the output for padded points can be discarded.

## TensorRT 部署方案（完整版）

### 一、已验证的环境信息

| 项目 | 值 | 影响 |
|------|-----|------|
| TensorRT | 8.6.1.6 @ `/usr/local/TensorRT-8.6.1.6` | 需设 `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` |
| CUDA | 11.8 @ `/usr/local/cuda-11.8` | 与 TRT 8.6 兼容 |
| GPU | NVIDIA L20 (Ada, sm_89) | 支持 FP16, TF32 |
| PyTorch | 2.2.2+cu118 | 可用于 GPU 内存管理 |
| ONNX opset | 16, 33 种算子 | TRT 8.6 全部支持 |

**ONNX 模型算子**（已验证 TRT 8.6 支持）：

```
Conv, Relu, Sigmoid, Add, Sub, Mul, Div, Pow, Sqrt, Reciprocal,
Concat, Transpose, Reshape, Expand, Unsqueeze, Slice, Gather, GatherElements,
ReduceMax, ReduceMean, ReduceSum, TopK, Where, Equal, GreaterOrEqual, LessOrEqual,
Cast, Identity, Shape, Constant, ConstantOfShape, If, RandomUniformLike
```

⚠️ **`RandomUniformLike`** 是唯一需要注意的算子 — TRT 8.6 支持，但不同 seed 行为可能略有差异，不影响推理正确性。

**TensorRT 8.6.1 API 关键方法：**

| 类别 | 方法 | 用途 |
|------|------|------|
| 构建 | `builder.create_network(EXPLICIT_BATCH)` | 创建网络 |
| 构建 | `trt.OnnxParser(network, logger)` | 解析 ONNX |
| 构建 | `profile.set_shape(name, min, opt, max)` | 动态 shape 优化 |
| 构建 | `config.set_memory_pool_limit(WORKSPACE, 2GB)` | 工作空间 |
| 构建 | `config.set_flag(BuilderFlag.FP16)` | FP16 推理 |
| 推理 | `context.set_input_shape(name, shape)` | 设置动态 shape |
| 推理 | `context.set_tensor_address(name, ptr)` | 设置 GPU 内存指针 |
| 推理 | `context.execute_async_v3(stream)` | CUDA stream 异步推理 |
| 获取 | `engine.get_tensor_name(i)` / `get_tensor_dtype(name)` | 查询 I/O 信息 |

### 二、数据特征与优化配置

每个 PLY 文件约 3000-3500 点，体素化后每 sub-cloud 的 N 在 1~3400 之间。

```
坐标范围: x=[-180, 380], y=[-175, 380], z=[-100, 80]
voxel_size: 0.1
每个 PLY 文件: ~3400 个点，唯一体素约 3000-3400 个
每个 sub-cloud: N ∈ [1, ~3400]
```

**优化 profile 设计：**

| Profile | npoints | 依据 |
|---------|---------|------|
| min | 64 | 模型最深层降采样 4^4=256，N≥64 安全 |
| opt | 4096 | 匹配 ONNX 导出时的 typical shape |
| max | 30000 | 覆盖最大场景（约 10x 典型文件） |

### 三、文件结构

```
deploy/
├── trt_utils.py              # 核心：TRT 引擎加载、GPU 内存管理、推理封装
├── trt_build.py              # 构建：ONNX → TRT .engine
├── trt_inference.py          # 推理：完整管线（PLY→体素化→TRT→投票合并）
├── trt_model_fp32.engine     # 生成物：FP32 引擎
└── trt_model_fp16.engine     # 生成物：FP16 引擎（可选）
```

### 四、`trt_utils.py` — 核心工具层

```python
class TRTSession:
    """
    封装 TRT 引擎加载、动态 shape、GPU 缓冲区管理。

    Usage:
        sess = TRTSession("deploy/trt_model_fp32.engine")
        output = sess.run(pos_np, x_np)  # pos: (1,N,3) float32, x: (1,4,N) float32
    """

    def __init__(self, engine_path):
        # 1. 加载引擎
        runtime = trt.Runtime(trt.Logger(trt.Logger.WARNING))
        with open(engine_path, "rb") as f:
            self.engine = runtime.deserialize_cuda_engine(f.read())
        self.context = self.engine.create_execution_context()

        # 2. 发现 I/O 张量
        self.inputs = {}   # {name: (shape, dtype, mode)}
        self.outputs = {}

        for i in range(self.engine.num_io_tensors):
            name = self.engine.get_tensor_name(i)
            mode = self.engine.get_tensor_mode(name)  # INPUT / OUTPUT
            dtype = self.engine.get_tensor_dtype(name)
            shape = self.engine.get_tensor_shape(name)
            info = {"shape": shape, "dtype": dtype, "name": name}
            if mode == trt.TensorIOMode.INPUT:
                self.inputs[name] = info
            else:
                self.outputs[name] = info

        # 3. 预分配 GPU 缓冲区
        self._gpu_buffers = {}  # {name: torch.Tensor (on CUDA)}

    def _allocate_output(self, name, shape):
        """按需分配输出 buffer（shape 随动态输入变化）"""
        info = self.outputs[name]
        trt_dtype = info["dtype"]
        torch_dtype = TRT_TO_TORCH[trt_dtype]
        tensor = torch.empty(tuple(shape), dtype=torch_dtype, device="cuda")
        self._gpu_buffers[name] = tensor
        self.context.set_tensor_address(name, tensor.data_ptr())
        return tensor

    def run(self, pos_np, x_np):
        """
        Args:
            pos_np: (1, N, 3) float32 numpy
            x_np:   (1, 4, N) float32 numpy

        Returns:
            output: (1, 2, N) float32 numpy
        """
        N = pos_np.shape[1]

        # 1. 设置动态 shape
        self.context.set_input_shape("pos", (1, N, 3))
        self.context.set_input_shape("x", (1, 4, N))

        # 2. 将输入拷贝到 GPU
        pos_gpu = torch.from_numpy(pos_np).cuda(non_blocking=True)
        x_gpu = torch.from_numpy(x_np).cuda(non_blocking=True)

        # 3. 设置输入指针
        self.context.set_tensor_address("pos", pos_gpu.data_ptr())
        self.context.set_tensor_address("x", x_gpu.data_ptr())

        # 4. 推断输出 shape 并分配
        # TensorRT 8.6: infer_shapes 可根据输入 shape 推断输出 shape
        if not self.context.all_binding_shapes_specified:
            self.context.infer_shapes()
        out_shape = self.context.get_tensor_shape("output")
        out_gpu = self._allocate_output("output", out_shape)

        # 5. 异步执行
        stream = torch.cuda.current_stream()
        self.context.execute_async_v3(stream.cuda_stream)
        stream.synchronize()

        # 6. 返回 CPU numpy
        return out_gpu.cpu().numpy()
```

**关键设计决策：**

| 决策 | 理由 |
|------|------|
| 用 `torch.empty(device='cuda')` 管理 GPU 内存 | 不依赖 cupy/pycuda，与已有环境兼容 |
| 每次 `run()` 调用 `set_input_shape` | 动态 N，每次推理前必须设置 |
| `execute_async_v3` + `stream.synchronize()` | 利用 CUDA stream 重叠传输和计算 |
| `infer_shapes()` 推断输出 shape | 不硬编码输出大小，兼容动态 N |

### 五、`trt_build.py` — 引擎构建

```python
"""
构建流程：
1. 设置 LD_LIBRARY_PATH
2. 解析 ONNX 模型
3. 配置动态 shape profile
4. 可选 FP16 标志
5. 序列化保存 .engine 文件

预计耗时：2-10 分钟（FP32），5-15 分钟（FP16）
"""

def build_engine(onnx_path, engine_path, fp16=False, min_n=64, opt_n=4096, max_n=30000):
    EXPLICIT_BATCH = 1 << int(trt.NetworkDefinitionCreationFlag.EXPLICIT_BATCH)

    builder = trt.Builder(trt.Logger(trt.Logger.WARNING))
    network = builder.create_network(EXPLICIT_BATCH)
    parser = trt.OnnxParser(network, trt.Logger(trt.Logger.WARNING))

    # 解析 ONNX
    with open(onnx_path, "rb") as f:
        if not parser.parse(f.read()):
            for i in range(parser.num_errors):
                print(f"  Parser error: {parser.get_error(i)}")
            raise RuntimeError("ONNX parsing failed")

    # 优化 profile（唯一动态维度：npoints）
    profile = builder.create_optimization_profile()
    profile.set_shape("pos", (1, min_n, 3), (1, opt_n, 3), (1, max_n, 3))
    profile.set_shape("x",   (1, 4, min_n), (1, 4, opt_n), (1, 4, max_n))

    # Builder 配置
    config = builder.create_builder_config()
    config.add_optimization_profile(profile)
    config.set_memory_pool_limit(trt.MemoryPoolType.WORKSPACE, 2 << 30)  # 2 GB

    if fp16:
        config.set_flag(trt.BuilderFlag.FP16)
        # STRICT_TYPES: 只在安全的地方用 FP16，保持精度
        # 不设置则 TRT 自动选择 FP16/FP32
        # 对于精度敏感场景建议不设置

    # 构建（耗时的步骤）
    print("  Building TRT engine (this may take several minutes)...")
    serialized = builder.build_serialized_network(network, config)
    if serialized is None:
        raise RuntimeError("Engine build failed (returned None)")

    with open(engine_path, "wb") as f:
        f.write(serialized)

    size_mb = len(serialized) / 1024 / 1024
    print(f"  Engine saved: {engine_path} ({size_mb:.1f} MB)")
```

**参数说明：**

| 参数 | 默认值 | 说明 |
|------|--------|------|
| `--fp16` | False | 启用 FP16 推理（速度 ~2x，精度损失 <0.5%） |
| `--min_n` | 64 | 最小输入点数（必须满足模型深层降采样要求） |
| `--opt_n` | 4096 | 优化目标点数 |
| `--max_n` | 30000 | 最大输入点数 |

**使用方式：**
```bash
# FP32 engine
LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/usr/local/TensorRT-8.6.1.6/targets/x86_64-linux-gnu/lib:/usr/local/cuda-11.8/lib64 \
python deploy/trt_build.py

# FP16 engine
LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/usr/local/TensorRT-8.6.1.6/targets/x86_64-linux-gnu/lib:/usr/local/cuda-11.8/lib64 \
python deploy/trt_build.py --fp16

# 自定义 profile
LD_LIBRARY_PATH=... python deploy/trt_build.py --min_n 128 --opt_n 8192 --max_n 65536
```

### 六、`trt_inference.py` — 推理管线

```python
"""
完整推理管线（复用 onnx_inference.py 的预处理逻辑）

Usage:
    python deploy/trt_inference.py --engine deploy/trt_model_fp32.engine --num_files 5 --compare
"""

def main():
    setup_trt_env()

    # 1. 加载 TRT 引擎
    session = TRTSession(args.engine)

    # 2. 加载统计量
    feat_mean, feat_std, z_mean, z_std = load_stats(stats_file)

    # 3. （可选）加载对比模型
    if args.compare:
        onnx_sess = ort.InferenceSession("deploy/onnx_model.onnx")
        pt_model = load_and_patch_pytorch_model()

    # 4. 遍历文件 + 体素级推理 + 投票合并
    for fname in test_files:
        coord, feat, label = load_data_ply(data_path)
        coord, feat, idx_points = preprocess_test(coord, feat, voxel_size=0.1)

        all_logits = []
        all_idx = []
        for idx_part in idx_points:
            # 预处理（同 onnx_inference.py）
            pos_batch, x_batch = preprocess_subcloud(
                coord, feat, idx_part, feat_mean, feat_std, z_mean, z_std
            )

            # N < min_n 时 padding（避免 TRT 引擎降采样失败）
            N = pos_batch.shape[1]
            if N < 64:
                pos_batch, x_batch = pad_to_min(pos_batch, x_batch, 64)

            # TRT 推理
            logits = session.run(pos_batch, x_batch)

            # 去 padding
            if N < 64:
                logits = logits[:, :, :N]

            all_logits.append(torch.from_numpy(logits[0]))
            all_idx.append(torch.from_numpy(idx_part).long())

        # 投票合并
        merged = scatter_mean(torch.cat(all_logits, dim=1).T, torch.cat(all_idx), dim=0)
        pred = merged.argmax(dim=1).numpy()
        acc = (pred == label).mean()
        print(f"  TRT acc={acc:.4f}")
```

**Padding 策略（处理 N < min_n）：**

```python
def pad_to_min(pos, x, min_n):
    """Duplicate last point to reach min_n."""
    N = pos.shape[1]
    if N >= min_n:
        return pos, x
    pad = min_n - N
    pos_pad = np.concatenate([pos, np.tile(pos[:, -1:, :], (1, pad, 1))], axis=1)
    x_pad = np.concatenate([x, np.tile(x[:, :, -1:], (1, 1, pad))], axis=2)
    return pos_pad.astype(np.float32), x_pad.astype(np.float32)
```

### 七、对比验证矩阵

推理脚本将同时对比三个后端：

| 对比项 | 方法 |
|--------|------|
| TRT vs PyTorch | `--compare` 标志同时加载三种后端 |
| FP32 vs FP16 | 分别加载两个 engine 对比 |
| TRT vs ONNX Runtime | 加载 ONNX 模型用 onnxruntime 推理 |

输出格式：
```
[file_001] TRT_FP32 acc=0.9123  TRT_FP16 acc=0.9121  ONNX acc=0.9130  PT acc=0.9225
           TRT_FP32 vs PT: pred_match=0.9412  logit_diff=12.34
           TRT_FP16 vs FP32: pred_match=0.9998  logit_diff=0.0012
```

### 八、风险与缓解

| 风险 | 影响 | 缓解措施 |
|------|------|----------|
| `RandomUniformLike` 在 TRT 行为不同 | FPS 随机采样不同 | 不影响正确性，仅精度波动 <0.5% |
| N < min_n 导致降采样失败 | 推理崩溃 | Python 端 padding 到 min_n |
| FP16 精度损失 | 少数边界点分类错误 | 与 FP32 对比，差异通常 <0.1% |
| TRT 构建 OOM | 构建失败 | 限制 workpace=2GB，max_n=30000 |
| `libnvinfer.so.8` 找不到 | 导入失败 | `setup_trt_env()` 设置 `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` |
| CUDA stream 冲突 | 结果错误 | `stream.synchronize()` 确保完成 |

### 九、预期性能

基于 L20 GPU (48GB, sm_89, ~65 TFLOPS FP16)：

| 精度 | 单次推理延迟 | 每文件推理（~3000 点） |
|------|-------------|----------------------|
| ONNX Runtime CPU | ~500ms | ~1.5s |
| TRT FP32 | ~5ms | ~0.15s (10x 加速) |
| TRT FP16 | ~2ms | ~0.06s (25x 加速) |

inference with dynamic point count.\n\n        Args:\n            pos: numpy array of shape (1, N, 3), dtype float32\n            x:   numpy array of shape (1, 4, N), dtype float32\n\n        Returns:\n            output: numpy array of shape (1, 2, N), dtype float32\n        \"\"\"\n        assert pos.ndim == 3 and pos.shape[0] == 1, f\"pos must be (1, N, 3), got {pos.shape}\"\n        assert x.ndim == 3 and x.shape[0] == 1, f\"x must be (1, 4, N), got {x.shape}\"\n        N = pos.shape[1]\n        assert x.shape[2] == N, f\"pos N ({N}) != x N ({x.shape[2]})\"\n\n        # --- set dynamic input shapes ---\n        self.context.set_input_shape(\"pos\", (1, N, 3))\n        self.context.set_input_shape(\"x\", (1, 4, N))\n\n        # --- transfer inputs to GPU ---\n        if \"pos\" not in self._input_buffers or self._input_buffers[\"pos\"].shape != pos.shape:\n            self._input_buffers[\"pos\"] = torch.empty(pos.shape, dtype=torch.float32, device=\"cuda\")\n        if \"x\" not in self._input_buffers or self._input_buffers[\"x\"].shape != x.shape:\n            self._input_buffers[\"x\"] = torch.empty(x.shape, dtype=torch.float32, device=\"cuda\")\n\n        pos_gpu = self._input_buffers[\"pos\"]\n        x_gpu = self._input_buffers[\"x\"]\n        pos_gpu.copy_(torch.from_numpy(pos), non_blocking=True)\n        x_gpu.copy_(torch.from_numpy(x), non_blocking=True)\n\n        # --- bind input pointers ---\n        self.context.set_tensor_address(\"pos\", pos_gpu.data_ptr())\n        self.context.set_tensor_address(\"x\", x_gpu.data_ptr())\n\n        # --- allocate / reuse output buffer ---\n        out_shape = (1, 2, N)\n        if self._output_buffer is None or self._output_buffer.shape != out_shape:\n            torch_dtype = TRT_TO_TORCH.get(self._output_dtype, torch.float32)\n            self._output_buffer = torch.empty(out_shape, dtype=torch_dtype, device=\"cuda\")\n        self.context.set_tensor_address(self._output_name, self._output_buffer.data_ptr())\n\n        # --- execute on current CUDA stream ---\n        stream = torch.cuda.current_stream()\n        self.context.execute_async_v3(stream.cuda_stream)\n        stream.synchronize()\n\n        # --- return as CPU numpy ---\n        return self._output_buffer.cpu().numpy()\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        info = [f\"TRTSession(engine={self._engine_path!r})\"]\n        for name, spec in self._input_specs.items():\n            info.append(f\"  input  {name}: {spec['shape']} ({spec['dtype']})\")\n        info.append(f\"  output {self._output_name}: {self._output_shape} ({self._output_dtype})\")\n        return \"\\n\".join(info)\n",

Create builder & network ----------\n    builder = trt.Builder(logger)\n    EXPLICIT_BATCH = 1 << int(trt.NetworkDefinitionCreationFlag.EXPLICIT_BATCH)\n    network = builder.create_network(EXPLICIT_BATCH)\n    parser = trt.OnnxParser(network, logger)\n\n    # ---------- 2. Parse ONNX ----------\n    print(f\"  Parsing ONNX: {onnx_path}\")\n    with open(onnx_path, \"rb\") as f:\n        onnx_data = f.read()\n\n    if not parser.parse(onnx_data):\n        errors = []\n        for i in range(parser.num_errors):\n            err = parser.get_error(i)\n            errors.append(f\"    [{err.code()}] {err.desc()}\")\n        raise RuntimeError(\n            f\"ONNX parsing failed with {parser.num_errors} error(s):\\n\" + \"\\n\".join(errors)\n        )\n\n    print(f\"  ONNX parsed OK.  Inputs: {network.num_inputs}, outputs: {network.num_outputs}\")\n\n    # ---------- 3. Dynamic shape optimization profile ----------\n    # Only 'npoints' dimension is dynamic (batch=1, channels=3/4 fixed)\n    profile = builder.create_optimization_profile()\n\n    # pos: (1, N, 3) — dynamic on dim 1\n    profile.set_shape(\"pos\",\n                      (1, min_n, 3),\n                      (1, opt_n, 3),\n                      (1, max_n, 3))\n\n    # x: (1, 4, N) — dynamic on dim 2\n    profile.set_shape(\"x\",\n                      (1, 4, min_n),\n                      (1, 4, opt_n),\n                      (1, 4, max_n))\n\n    # ---------- 4. Builder configuration ----------\n    config = builder.create_builder_config()\n    config.add_optimization_profile(profile)\n    config.set_memory_pool_limit(trt.MemoryPoolType.WORKSPACE, int(workspace_gb * 1024**3))\n\n    # Enable TF32 (available on L20 / Ampere+)\n    if hasattr(config, 'set_flag') and hasattr(trt.BuilderFlag, 'TF32'):\n        # TF32 is enabled by default on Ampere; set it explicitly\n        pass\n\n    if fp16:\n        config.set_flag(trt.BuilderFlag.FP16)\n        print(\"  FP16 mode: ENABLED\")\n\n    print(f\"  Dynamic profile: min_n={min_n}, opt_n={opt_n}, max_n={max_n}\")\n    print(f\"  Workspace: {workspace_gb} GiB\")\n    print(f\"  Building engine (this may take several minutes)...\")\n\n    # ---------- 5. Build ----------\n    serialized = builder.build_serialized_network(network, config)\n    if serialized is None:\n        raise RuntimeError(\"Engine build failed (returned None). \"\n                           \"This may be caused by an unsupported ONNX op, \"\n                           \"insufficient workspace, or driver mismatch.\")\n\n    # ---------- 6. Save ----------\n    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(engine_path) or \".\", exist_ok=True)\n    with open(engine_path, \"wb\") as f:\n        f.write(serialized)\n\n    size_mb = len(serialized) / 1024 / 1024\n    print(f\"  Engine saved: {engine_path} ({size_mb:.1f} MB)\")\n    return serialized\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        description=\"Build TensorRT engine from ONNX model (TRT 8.6.1+)\"\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\"--onnx\", type=str, default=\"deploy/onnx_model.onnx\",\n                        help=\"Path to input ONNX model\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--output\", type=str, default=None,\n                        help=\"Output engine path (default: trt_model_fp{32,16}.engine)\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--fp16\", action=\"store_true\",\n                        help=\"Enable FP16 inference (approx 2x speedup)\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--min_n\", type=int, default=64,\n                        help=\"Minimum point count for dynamic profile\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--opt_n\", type=int, default=4096,\n                        help=\"Optimal point count for dynamic profile\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--max_n\", type=int, default=30000,\n                        help=\"Maximum point count for dynamic profile\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--workspace\", type=int, default=2,\n                        help=\"GPU workspace in GiB\")\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    if args.output is None:\n        suffix = \"fp16\" if args.fp16 else \"fp32\"\n        args.output = f\"deploy/trt_model_{suffix}.engine\"\n\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"HPENet V2 → TensorRT Engine Builder (TRT 8.6)\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(f\"  ONNX:   {args.onnx}\")\n    print(f\"  Output: {args.output}\")\n    print(f\"  FP16:   {args.fp16}\")\n    print(f\"  Profile: min_n={args.min_n}, opt_n={args.opt_n}, max_n={args.max_n}\")\n\n    # Build environment requires TRT libraries\n    setup_trt_env()\n\n    build_engine(\n        onnx_path=args.onnx,\n        engine_path=args.output,\n        fp16=args.fp16,\n        min_n=args.min_n,\n        opt_n=args.opt_n,\n        max_n=args.max_n,\n        workspace_gb=args.workspace,\n    )\n\n    print(\"\\nDone!\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n",

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"\nTensorRT inference pipeline for HPENet V2 radar semantic segmentation.\n\nFeatures:\n  - Full voxel voting pipeline (same preprocessing as onnx_inference.py)\n  - GPU-accelerated inference via TensorRT 8.6\n  - Comparison with PyTorch and ONNX Runtime backends\n\nUsage:\n    # Basic TRT inference\n    python deploy/trt_inference.py --engine deploy/trt_model_fp32.engine\n\n    # Compare all three backends\n    python deploy/trt_inference.py --engine deploy/trt_model_fp32.engine --compare\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport sys\n\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))\n\nimport time\nimport argparse\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom plyfile import PlyData\nfrom tqdm import tqdm\n\nfrom openpoints.dataset.data_util import voxelize\nfrom openpoints.utils.config import EasyConfig\nfrom openpoints.models.build import build_model_from_cfg\nfrom deploy.onnx_backend import patch_model_for_onnx\nfrom deploy.trt_utils import setup_trt_env, TRTSession\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#  Data loading & preprocessing (identical to onnx_inference.py)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef load_data_ply(data_path):\n    \"\"\"Load a single radar PLY file.\n\n    Returns:\n        coord: (N, 3)  xyz\n        feat:  (N, 3)  rcs, snr, v\n        label: (N,)    ground truth\n    \"\"\"\n    plydata = PlyData.read(data_path)\n    vertex = plydata[\"vertex\"].data\n\n    required = [\"x\", \"y\", \"z\", \"rcs\", \"snr\", \"v\", \"label\"]\n    for f in required:\n        if f not in vertex.dtype.names:\n            raise ValueError(f\"Field '{f}' not found in {data_path}\")\n\n    data = np.column_stack((\n        vertex[\"x\"], vertex[\"y\"], vertex[\"z\"],\n        vertex[\"rcs\"], vertex[\"snr\"], vertex[\"v\"],\n        vertex[\"label\"],\n    )).astype(np.float32)\n    data = np.nan_to_num(data, nan=0.0)\n\n    coord = data[:, :3]\n    feat = data[:, 3:6]\n    label = data[:, 6]\n    return coord, feat, label\n\n\ndef preprocess_test(coord, feat, voxel_size=0.1):\n    \"\"\"Voxelize → split into sub-clouds (one point per voxel per shift).\"\"\"\n    coord = coord - coord.min(0)\n\n    idx_points = []\n    voxel_idx = None\n    reverse_idx_part = None\n    reverse_idx_sort = None\n\n    if voxel_size is not None:\n        idx_sort, voxel_idx, count = voxelize(coord, voxel_size, mode=1)\n        for i in range(count.max()):\n            idx_select = np.cumsum(np.insert(count, 0, 0)[0:-1]) + i % count\n            idx_part = idx_sort[idx_select]\n            np.random.shuffle(idx_part)\n            idx_points.append(idx_part)\n    else:\n        idx_points.append(np.arange(coord.shape[0]))\n\n    coord = np.nan_to_num(coord, nan=0.0)\n    feat = np.nan_to_num(feat, nan=0.0)\n    return coord, feat, idx_points, voxel_idx, reverse_idx_part, reverse_idx_sort\n\n\ndef load_stats(stats_file):\n    \"\"\"Load feature normalization statistics.\"\"\"\n    stats = torch.load(stats_file, map_location=\"cpu\")\n    return (\n        stats[\"feat_mean\"],   # (3,)\n        stats[\"feat_std\"],    # (3,)\n        stats[\"z_mean\"],      # scalar\n        stats[\"z_std\"],       # scalar\n    )\n\n\ndef preprocess_subcloud(coord, feat, idx_part, feat_mean, feat_std,\n                        z_mean, z_std, gravity_dim=2):\n    \"\"\"Prepare a single sub-cloud for model inference.\n\n    Returns:\n        pos_batch: (1, N_part, 3) float32 numpy\n        x_batch:   (1, 4, N_part) float32 numpy\n    \"\"\"\n    coord_part = coord[idx_part].copy()\n    coord_part -= coord_part.min(0)\n\n    feat_part = feat[idx_part].copy()\n\n    pos_t = torch.from_numpy(coord_part).float()\n    pos_t = pos_t - pos_t.mean(dim=0, keepdim=True)\n    pos_t[:, gravity_dim] -= pos_t[:, gravity_dim].min()\n\n    feat_t = torch.from_numpy(feat_part).float()\n    heights_t = pos_t[:, gravity_dim:gravity_dim + 1]\n\n    feat_t = (feat_t - feat_mean) / feat_std.clamp(min=1e-5)\n    heights_t = (heights_t - z_mean) / z_std.clamp(min=1e-5)\n\n    x_combined = torch.cat([feat_t, heights_t], dim=-1)\n\n    pos_batch = pos_t.unsqueeze(0).numpy().astype(np.float32)\n    x_batch = x_combined.unsqueeze(0).transpose(1, 2).contiguous().numpy().astype(np.float32)\n    return pos_batch, x_batch\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#  Padding helper (handle N < min_n for TRT)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef pad_subcloud(pos, x, min_n):\n    \"\"\"Pad to min_n points by replicating the last point.\"\"\"\n    N = pos.shape[1]\n    if N >= min_n:\n        return pos, x\n    pad = min_n - N\n    pos_pad = np.concatenate(\n        [pos, np.tile(pos[:, -1:, :], (1, pad, 1))], axis=1\n    ).astype(np.float32)\n    x_pad = np.concatenate(\n        [x, np.tile(x[:, :, -1:], (1, 1, pad))], axis=2\n    ).astype(np.float32)\n    return pos_pad, x_pad\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#  Inference loops\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef infer_one_cloud_trt(session, coord, feat, idx_points, feat_mean, feat_std,\n                        z_mean, z_std, min_n=64, gravity_dim=2):\n    \"\"\"TRT inference on all sub-clouds of one point cloud.\"\"\"\n    all_logits = []\n    all_idx = []\n\n    for idx_part in idx_points:\n        pos_batch, x_batch = preprocess_subcloud(\n            coord, feat, idx_part, feat_mean, feat_std, z_mean, z_std, gravity_dim,\n        )\n\n        N_true = pos_batch.shape[1]\n        pos_batch, x_batch = pad_subcloud(pos_batch, x_batch, min_n)\n\n        logits = session.run(pos_batch, x_batch)  # (1, 2, N_padded)\n\n        # Trim padding\n        if N_true < min_n:\n            logits = logits[:, :, :N_true]\n\n        all_logits.append(torch.from_numpy(logits[0]))  # (2, N_part)\n        all_idx.append(torch.from_numpy(idx_part).long())\n\n    # Merge with scatter mean\n    all_logits_cat = torch.cat(all_logits, dim=1)\n    all_logits_cat = all_logits_cat.transpose(0, 1)\n    idx_flat = torch.cat(all_idx, dim=0)\n\n    from torch_scatter import scatter\n    merged = scatter(all_logits_cat, idx_flat, dim=0, reduce=\"mean\")\n    return merged\n\n\ndef infer_one_cloud_onnx(session, coord, feat, idx_points, feat_mean, feat_std,\n                         z_mean, z_std, gravity_dim=2):\n    \"\"\"ONNX Runtime inference on all sub-clouds.\"\"\"\n    all_logits = []\n    all_idx = []\n\n    for idx_part in idx_points:\n        pos_batch, x_batch = preprocess_subcloud(\n            coord, feat, idx_part, feat_mean, feat_std, z_mean, z_std, gravity_dim,\n        )\n\n        outputs = session.run(None, {\"pos\": pos_batch, \"x\": x_batch})\n        logits = outputs[0]\n\n        all_logits.append(torch.from_numpy(logits[0]))\n        all_idx.append(torch.from_numpy(idx_part).long())\n\n    all_logits_cat = torch.cat(all_logits, dim=1).transpose(0, 1)\n    idx_flat = torch.cat(all_idx, dim=0)\n\n    from torch_scatter import scatter\n    merged = scatter(all_logits_cat, idx_flat, dim=0, reduce=\"mean\")\n    return merged\n\n\ndef infer_one_cloud_pytorch(model, coord, feat, idx_points, feat_mean, feat_std,\n                            z_mean, z_std, gravity_dim=2):\n    \"\"\"PyTorch model inference on all sub-clouds.\"\"\"\n    all_logits = []\n    all_idx = []\n\n    for idx_part in idx_points:\n        pos_batch, x_batch = preprocess_subcloud(\n            coord, feat, idx_part, feat_mean, feat_std, z_mean, z_std, gravity_dim,\n        )\n\n        pos_t = torch.from_numpy(pos_batch)\n        x_t = torch.from_numpy(x_batch)\n\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            logits = model({\"pos\": pos_t, \"x\": x_t})\n\n        all_logits.append(logits.squeeze(0))\n scatter\n    merged = scatter(all_logits_cat, idx_flat, dim=0, reduce=\"mean\")\n    return merged\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#  Main\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\"HPENet V2 TensorRT Inference\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--engine\", type=str, default=\"deploy/trt_model_fp32.engine\",\n                        help=\"Path to TensorRT engine\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--onnx\", type=str, default=\"deploy/onnx_model.onnx\",\n                        help=\"Path to ONNX model (for comparison)\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--checkpoint\", type=str,\n                        default=\"log/radar/radar-train-hpenet-l-ngpus1-20260515-013127-HXWMALkaAC4GiUWjNV5c3g/checkpoint/radar-train-hpenet-l-ngpus1-20260515-013127-HXWMALkaAC4GiUWjNV5c3g_ckpt_best.pth\",\n                        help=\"Path to PyTorch checkpoint (for comparison)\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--data_dir\", type=str,\n                        default=\"data/RadarClassi/radarfull/raw\",\n parser.add_argument(\"--compare\", action=\"store_true\",\n                        help=\"Compare TRT vs ONNX vs PyTorch outputs\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--min_n\", type=int, default=64,\n I need to add a monkey-patch for `InvResMLP_block.forward` to remove the `if f.shape[-1] == identity.shape[-1]` conditional. Let me add this to `patch_model_for_onnx`.
```

The original forward:
```python
def forward(self, pf):
    p, f, dp, idx_dp, pe = pf
    identity = f
    f = self.convs([p, f, dp, idx_dp, pe])
    f = self.pwconv(f)
    if f.shape[-1] == identity.shape[-1] and self.use_res:
        f += identity
    f = self.act(f)
    return [p, f, dp, idx_dp, pe]
```

The patched version should be:
```python
def forward(self, pf):
    p, f, dp, idx_dp, pe = pf
    identity = f
    f = self.convs([p, f, dp, idx_dp, pe])
    f = self.pwconv(f)
    # Always add residual (the condition is always True for stride-1 blocks)
    f = f + identity
    f = self.act(f)
    return [p, f, dp, idx_dp, pe]
```

But wait, `self.use_res` might be False in some cases. Looking at the InvResMLP_block creation in InvResMLP:


And `InvResMLP` has `self.use_res = use_res` which defaults to True from kwargs (which defaults to True). So `self.use_res` is always True.

But even if it were False, we'd need to handle both cases. Actually, in eval mode with this config, it's always True.

However, a simpler approach: just monkey-patch the `forward` method on each `InvResMLP_block` instance after model building. The `if` check is equivalent to always True for these blocks.

```python
def traceable_random_fps(xyz, npoint):
    B, N, _ = xyz.shape
    # Deterministic sampling: take every (N/npoint)th point, with an offset
    step = N // max(1, npoint)
    offset = torch.randint(0, max(1, step), (B, 1), device=xyz.device)
    idx = (offset + step * torch.arange(npoint, device=xyz.device).unsqueeze(0)) % N
    return idx.long()
```

```python
    step = max(1, N // max(1, npoint))

```
